# Plan: rebuild the Read, Recall, Colour section launch surface
_Round 4 — revised by Claude after four Codex rounds_

Approved mockup: `docs/design-previews/rrc-section-launch-chamber.html`
(measured 1203px product surface with the drawer open, versus 2778px for the
page it replaces).

## Goal

A student opening a Read, Recall, Colour **section** currently lands on
`ActivityIntro`, a 2778px editorial page built for the typed Activities: two orb
circles, a rules list, a six-section briefing, and a Start button below the fold
that needs a sticky dock to compensate. The approved redesign replaces it with a
single ~1200px card that fits a laptop without scrolling: a stat strip of four
derived numbers, the four method steps as a segmented control that opens one at
a time, a run-history table with per-stimulus tick rows, and Start in the
top-right where it never needs a dock.

Scoped to **section mode only**. The eight typed Activities and the
section-library door keep the page they have.

## Release prerequisite (blocking)

Multi-run history depends on `20260903190000_a_report_card_per_attempt.sql`,
which drops the single-row primary key and adds `attempt_no`. If that column is
absent in prod, `listMyRecentSectionGrades`'s select fails, `isUnknownColumn`
swallows it and the helper returns `[]` — so there is **no graceful degradation
available**; the page would simply look like a student who has never run
anything. Round 1's "show the single most recent run and say so" was not
implementable. Confirm the migration is applied in production **before** this
ships. If it is not, applying it is part of this work, not a follow-up.

## Approach

### Structure

1. **Split `ActivityIntro`, do not early-return inside it.** The component calls
   `useReducedMotion`, `useRef`, two `useState`s and a `useEffect` near the top;
   a `mode === "section"` return placed before them would be a conditional hook
   and would break the moment mode changes without a remount. Instead:
   `ActivityIntro` becomes a hook-free wrapper that picks between
   `ActivityEditorialIntro` (today's body, moved verbatim, hooks intact) and the
   new `SectionLaunch`. Existing `ActivityIntro.*.test.tsx` assertions keep
   passing because they render through the wrapper.

2. **Do not use the name "Chamber" in code.** `worlds/chamber/` already exists as
   a different concept, and ADR 0009 says RRC is explicitly not a world. The
   nickname stays in `docs/design-previews/`; the identifier is `SectionLaunch`,
   the CSS namespace `.sl-`.

### Data

3. **Extend `listMyRecentSectionGrades` with a section filter, applied before the
   limit.** As it stands the helper orders and limits across *every* section, so
   filtering client-side can return zero runs for this section while real history
   exists just outside the window. Add an optional `sourceSection` argument
   applied as `.eq("source_section", …)` **before** `.order().limit(3)`. The
   existing no-argument callers are unaffected.

4. **Make failure distinguishable from emptiness.** The helper currently catches
   query errors and returns `[]`, which makes the "failed" state promised below
   unreachable. Give it a discriminated result (or a `throwOnError` option) so
   `SectionLaunch` can tell "nothing yet" from "we could not load this". Existing
   callers keep today's degrade-to-empty behaviour explicitly.

5. **`created_at` for ordering AND for the date shown.** `graded_at` is refreshed
   by a regrade, so a regraded old attempt overtakes a newer one and becomes
   "Last run", corrupting the trend. Tie-breaking cannot repair that. Add
   `created_at` to the select, order by it and then `(assignment_id, attempt_no desc)`,
   add it to the widened `Run` type, and render the history table's date column
   from it — otherwise ordering is stable while the dates beside it still move.

6. **Scope history by student id, not by which surface is rendering.** Admin RLS
   returns every student's rows, so no combination of su...

[external_agent_tool_result]
12. **The four beats are `sections[1].steps`, not `sections[]`.** Verified against
    `read-recall-colour-section.instructions.json`: the briefing has **six**
    sections, and Read it / Say it back / Colour it / The Recall are the four
    `steps` inside "What you actually do." The strip renders the first section
    that has `steps`; "The full briefing" tab renders all six as prose; and the
    mockup's **"why this beat" panel is cut**, because no beat-to-rationale
    mapping is authored anywhere and the mockup was borrowing an unrelated
    section heading. The drawer shows the step's own body and nothing invented.

13. **Fix the literal-asterisk leak.** `renderInlineMarks` covers `briefing.lede`,
    `briefing.closer` and `section.body[]` but not `section.heading`, `step.label`,
    `step.body` or `demo.beats[].text` (`ActivityIntro.tsx:294–318`). Route all
    four through it, in both components.

### Surface

14. **States, explicitly.** Loading (skeleton — an empty flash reads as "you have
    never run this"), loaded-with-runs, loaded-with-none, and failed (a short
    line, never a silent zero). No fetch when there is no student identity or
    `showHistory` is false. Cancel in flight on unmount; ignore stale resolutions.

15. **Accessibility — both switches, not just the strip.** The page has two
    panel-swapping controls: the four-beat segmented strip, and the inner
    "This beat / The full briefing" switch. Both get the complete tabs pattern —
    `role="tablist"` / `role="tab"` / `role="tabpanel"`, arrow-key roving focus,
    `aria-selected`, visible `:focus-visible` ring — with independent tablists, or
    the inner one becomes a plainly labelled disclosure button. Keyboard traversal
    is tested for whichever is chosen.

16. **Teal accent** behind the `.sl-` wrapper per `DESIGN.md` — no inline hex, no
    change to the global token set, no change to `recall/section.css`.

17. **Tests.** New `SectionLaunch.test.tsx`: stat cells derive from grades, not
    constants; trend sign correct in both directions; an `ungraded` stimulus
    renders as not-judged and *counts* as recalled; a run with no reliable slot
    count draws no tick row and is excluded from the aggregate; ticks place by
    `item_order`; the beat strip comes from `steps` and is absent when no section
    has any; history is not fetched when `showHistory` is false; a failed fetch
    renders the failure line rather than the empty state; arrow keys move between
    tabs; no em dash. Update the two `ActivityIntro.copy.test.tsx` cases asserting
    the `mode === "section"` how-line, since that branch moves.

18. **Verification gate.** Per `CLAUDE.md` this is not done on the diff: dev
    server, a real section loaded, screenshot, and the full matrix **before** the
    push. The matrix is three axes, not one: viewport (narrow / mid / wide /
    sidecar-docked), **theme (light and dark)**, and **`prefers-reduced-motion`**.
    The mockup carries dark-theme tokens and the component tree already honours
    `useReducedMotion`, so a surface approved in one state can ship broken in the
    other two.

## Rejected from Codex's critique, with reasons

- **"Start with the drawer closed; beat-1-open contradicts the mockup and may
  invalidate the no-scroll claim."** Wrong on both halves. The mockup's script
  ends in `open(2)` and the published page renders with beat 3 open; the 1203px
  measurement was taken in that state. Default stays open-on-first-beat.
- **"Write a new `student_id + source_section` query."** Superseded rather than
  adopted: the existing helper is extended with a section filter (step 3), which
  keeps one bounded, RLS-scoped read instead of two.

## Key decisions & tradeoffs

- **Wrapper split vs. rewrite.** `ActivityIntro` serves 9 Activities plus teacher
  preview. Moving today's body wholesale into `ActivityEditorialIntro` keeps every
  existing assertion valid and keeps hooks unconditional. Cost: one extra file.
- **Heavy-ish read...

-- The card and expanded instruction share this step body.
-- Include the library door as well as individual sections; leave completed
-- assignment snapshots unchanged.
do $$
declare
  v_read_step text := 'Read about one sentence at a time, paying attention to what it means. If that feels like too much to remember, start with a shorter phrase.';
begin
  update public.activities
     set instructions = jsonb_set(instructions,
       '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,body}', to_jsonb(v_read_step), false)
   where slug like 'read-recall-colour-%'
     and mode in ('section', 'section-library')
     and instructions #>> '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,label}' in ('Read it', 'Read it.');

  alter table public.activity_assignments disable trigger activity_assignments_guard_immutables;

  update public.activity_assignments aa
     set instructions = jsonb_set(aa.instructions,
       '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,body}', to_jsonb(v_read_step), false)
    from public.activities a
   where aa.activity_id = a.id
     and a.slug like 'read-recall-colour-%'
     and a.mode in ('section', 'section-library')
     and aa.status is distinct from 'completed'
     and aa.instructions #>> '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,label}' in ('Read it', 'Read it.');

  alter table public.activity_assignments enable trigger activity_assignments_guard_immutables;
end $$;

# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Project snapshot

LSAT U is a premium ed-tech web app: React 18 + Vite 5 + TypeScript + Tailwind + shadcn/ui, Supabase backend with Edge Functions, hosted on Lovable. The Lovable-connected project is the source of truth; GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`) is the mirror. Treat the connected project as canonical unless told otherwise.

## Second brain (product memory)

Product context, design decisions, pedagogy, and status do NOT live in this repo. They live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`. This repo is the code; the vault is the decision layer. Read the vault when starting a session, needing product/design/pedagogy context, or making a decision that touches roadmap or UX. Skip it for trivial code edits.

Path sanity check first:

- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`
- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`

If any older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat that as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.

Protocol is canonical in `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md` (Session Start / Session End), run via two skills:

- **`/pickup`** (start / "brief me"): reads `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry, then gives a 6-8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and waits.
- **`/wrapup`** (handoff / "done for today"): proposes which tasks are done (confirm first), strikes them through in `wiki/tasks.md`, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, appends a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, updates the task anchor, gives a 4-line confirmation.

`wiki/tasks.md` is the living ledger of Now / Next / Done; finished work is struck through with a date so a new session never re-runs it.

Where LSATU truth lives in the vault (paths relative to `~/ai-os`):

The vault was rebuilt on 2026-07-16. **Live** pages:

- Teaching voice (route ALL student-facing copy through it): `wiki/teaching-voice.md`

- A student's whole record (transcripts, notes, homework): `students/<name>/` — see `students/README.md` for the primary/mirror rule
- LSAT teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md` (fills by ingest)

**Archived** — the pre-rebuild product knowledge is read-only reference at `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`; it is not maintained and may be stale. Re-ingest a page through `raw/` if it becomes load-bearing again rather than editing it in place:

- Product context: `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md` (flagship Main Conclusion bootcamp)
- Design system (colors, type, role tokens): `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`

- Quality bar all bootcamps finish to: `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`
- Concepts, entities, domain index: `…/concepts/`, `…/entities/`, `…/index.md`

Rules: `raw/` is immutable input, never edit it. Read knowledge from `wiki/`, not `raw/`. Use the `obsidian` CLI (`search`, `backlinks`, `read`) for graph traversal (needs the Obsidian app open); flat file reads work without it. When saving deliverables to the vault, use `[[wiki-link]]` syntax and `UPPERCASE-HYPHENATED.md` naming.

# Guided Drill Assignment

Use this skill to turn student performance context into a verified guided drill assignment in LSAT U.

The operating standard is: do not merely recommend a drill. Choose the right drill from the student's recent transcript/context, verify the drill actually launches and is wired correctly, assign it through the platform, then verify the student's notification surface.

## Inputs

Expect one or more of:

- A student name or shorthand, such as `C`, `C. Alexander`, or another current student.
- A request to inspect a recent transcript, chat export, meeting note, student page, or platform record.
- A request to find an appropriate guided drill.
- A request to assign a drill and make sure the student is notified.

If the student identity is ambiguous and cannot be safely inferred from local context, ask one concise clarifying question before assigning anything.

## Required Context

For LSAT U vault work, follow the vault operating order before making a recommendation:

1. Read `wiki/hot.md`.
2. Read `projects/lsatu/README.md`.
3. Read `wiki/dashboard.md`.
4. Read the relevant student page under `students/`.
5. Read the student's `assignment-log.md` if it exists so you do not repeat assigned work unless Joshua explicitly asks for a repeat.
6. Read `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md` so already-verified drills are not re-QA'd unnecessarily.
7. Search all available transcripts, transcript downloads, chat exports, and meeting notes for that student.
8. Sort candidate transcript/context files by the actual meeting date, not file modification time, and use the most up-to-date transcript as the primary source of truth.

Prefer local sources first. Do not invent student weaknesses or assignment history. If an older transcript conflicts with a newer one, follow the newer transcript and mention the older file only as background.

Useful searches:

```bash
rg -n "student-name-or-shorthand|student full name|student email" wiki raw students projects
find students -path "*transcript*" -type f | rg -i "student-slug|student-name"
```

## Drill Selection

Choose the drill by matching the student's most recent need to the drill's pedagogical purpose.

Evaluate:

- Newest transcript evidence by meeting date: missed concepts, confusion, pacing, task avoidance, question type weakness, or reasoning pattern weakness.
- Student readiness: assign teach-first drills when the student needs instruction, not a cold test.
- Drill metadata: title, slug, week/module, question type, tags, pool size, question count, estimated time.
- Platform constraints: student must have classroom and drill access.
- Assignment history: avoid assigning a drill already present in the student's `assignment-log.md` unless Joshua explicitly wants a repeat or the log says it should be repeated.

When the best drill is not obvious, produce a short ranked recommendation before assigning:

```markdown
Recommended: <drill title>

# Activity Centre

The home for bespoke, coach-led skill exercises (e.g. Negation Station) that a student is assigned and works through conversationally. Distinct from the question-bank MCQ **Drill** engine: Activities are authored content with their own answer keys, not timed runs over `qid` questions.

## Language

**Activity Centre**:
The module that hosts and lists Activities. The student's home for assigned skill work that isn't question-bank drilling.
_Avoid_: Activities Centre, Skills Centre, Drill Centre

**Activity**:
One bespoke skill exercise — a titled, self-contained set of authored items with an answer key, worked through with a coach (e.g. "Negation Station"). The unit a teacher assigns.
_Avoid_: Skill drill, drill (reserved for the MCQ engine), station, module, game

**Homepage**:
Joshua-facing shorthand for an individual Activity's intro/start page: the page that shows the Activity title, item count, overview copy, example, and Start action before the student begins the first Question Set.
_Avoid_: LSAT U landing page, Foyer, Activity Centre list

**Question Set**:
Codex-facing term for one complete per-question unit inside an Activity: the Question Type, the prompt, the response area, the coach feedback, the credited answer, and the answer key material used to judge the response. In the UI, this should still be labeled "Question N"; when Joshua says "question set," he means this full unit, not the collection of all questions.
_Avoid_: Question collection, drill set, item bank

**Question Type**:
The classification pill inside a Question Set that names what kind of statement or reasoning form the student is working with (e.g. "Universal Statement," "Conditional Statement," "Causal Statement"). This is the canonical term for the label currently shown above the prompt.
_Avoid_: Category, tag, badge, label

**Drill**:
Reserved term. The existing question-bank engine: a timed run over multiple-choice questions pulled by `qid`. An Activity is NOT a Drill.

**Item**:
One prompt within an Activity that the student responds to (e.g. a single statement to negate). Ordered easy→hard as authored.
_Avoid_: Question (overloaded with the qid question bank), problem, task

**Credited answer**:
The author-supplied correct response to an Item, used as ground truth when the coach judges a student's response. Some Items carry several acceptable phrasings.
_Avoid_: Solution, key answer, right answer

**Coach**:
The conversational AI tutor that presents Items one at a time, reads the student's free-text response, and gives immediate supportive feedback against the credited answer. Built on the existing AI-gateway tutor pattern (`analyze-stem-response`).
_Avoid_: Tutor (reserved for the standalone Tutor surface), bot, grader

**Verdict**:
The coach's judgement of a single student response: `correct`, `partial`, or `incorrect`. `partial` captures a near-miss the Activity exists to teach (e.g. a polar-opposite instead of a true negation). Drives the assignment score and future weakness analytics.
_Avoid_: Grade, mark, result

**Activity assignment**:
A per-student issued copy of an Activity, snapshotting its content at assign time and tracking the student's progress/score. Mirrors the homework `assignment` pattern; immutable snapshot once issued.
_Avoid_: Homework (that's the MCQ-set pattern), task

### Read, Recall, Colour

The four-step vocabulary of the RRC runner, plus the language its grading uses.
The source method's own words for these steps are listed under `_Avoid_` and are
never used in the product.

**Chunk**:
An authored cut of a stimulus, roughly a sentence, carrying the Details that fall inside it. A guide for her eye and the unit content is written in, never a boundary she is held to: she reads, recalls and colours in whatever units she likes.
_Avoid_: Line, segment, piece, part

**Read** (step 1):
The silent reading of a chunk. Nothing is spoken.
_Avoid_: Skim, study

**Retelling** (step 2):
Saying a chunk back out...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

import { supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';
import { assignWork } from '@/lib/assignWork';
import { createClassroomItem } from '@/lib/classroomItems';
import { notifyStudentOfClassroomItem, type NotifyResult } from '@/lib/classroomNotify';
import type { Activity } from '@/features/activity-centre/api/types';
import type { GuidedDrill } from '@/lib/guidedDrills';

export interface AssignableResource {
  title: string;
  kind?: string | null;
  source_id?: string | null;
  url?: string | null;
  subtitle?: string | null;
  description?: string | null;
}

export interface ResourceAssignResult {
  ok: boolean;
  route: string;
  classroomItemId?: string;
  sourceAssignmentId?: string;
  reusedExisting?: boolean;
  notify: NotifyResult;
}

const UUID_RE = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[1-5][0-9a-f]{3}-[89ab][0-9a-f]{3}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;

function toPath(url?: string | null): string {
  if (!url) return '/classroom';
  try {
    const parsed = new URL(url, window.location.origin);
    return parsed.pathname + parsed.search + parsed.hash;
  } catch {
    return url.startsWith('/') ? url : '/classroom';
  }
}

function slugFromTitle(title: string) {
  return title.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-').replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '');
}

async function findActivity(resource: AssignableResource): Promise<Activity | null> {
  const sourceId = resource.source_id?.trim();
  if (sourceId) {
    const column = UUID_RE.test(sourceId) ? 'id' : 'slug';
    const { data, error } = await supabase.from('activities').select('*').eq(column, sourceId).maybeSingle();
    if (error) throw error;
    if (data) return data as unknown as Activity;
  }

  const slug = slugFromTitle(resource.title);
  const { data: bySlug, error: slugError } = await supabase.from('activities').select('*').eq('slug', slug).maybeSingle();
  if (slugError) throw slugError;
  if (bySlug) return bySlug as unknown as Activity;

  const { data, error } = await supabase
    .from('activities')
    .select('*')
    .ilike('title', `%${resource.title.trim()}%`)
    .limit(1)
    .maybeSingle();
  if (error) throw error;
  return data ? (data as unknown as Activity) : null;
}

async function findGuidedDrill(resource: AssignableResource): Promise<GuidedDrill | null> {
  const sourceId = resource.source_id?.trim();
  if (sourceId) {
    const column = UUID_RE.test(sourceId) ? 'id' : 'slug';
    const { data, error } = await supabase.from('guided_drills').select('*').eq(column, sourceId).maybeSingle();
    if (error) throw error;
    if (data) return data as GuidedDrill;
  }

  const slug = slugFromTitle(resource.title);
  const { data: bySlug, error: slugError } = await supabase.from('guided_drills').select('*').eq('slug', slug).maybeSingle();
  if (slugError) throw slugError;
  if (bySlug) return bySlug as GuidedDrill;

  const { data, error } = await supabase
    .from('guided_drills')
    .select('*')
    .ilike('title', `%${resource.title.trim()}%`)
    .limit(1)
    .maybeSingle();
  if (error) throw error;
  return data ? (data as GuidedDrill) : null;
}

async function ensureClassroomItem(input: {
  studentId: string;
  adminUserId: string;
  type: 'activity' | 'drill' | 'bootcamp_module' | 'note';
  title: string;
  description?: string | null;
  sourceTable?: string;
  sourceId?: string;
  route: string;
}) {
  if (input.sourceTable && input.sourceId) {
    const { data: existing, error } = await supabase
      .from('classroom_items')
      .select('id')
      .eq('student_id', input.studentId)
      .eq('source_table', input.sourceTable)
      .eq('source_id', input.sourceId)
      .neq('status', 'archived')
      .limit(1)
      .maybeSingle();
    if (error) throw error;
    if (existing?.id) return existing.id as string;
  }

  const item = await createClassroomItem({
    studentId: input.studentId,
    createdBy: input.adminUserId,
    type: input.type,
    title: input.title,
    description: input.description ?? undefined,
    sourceTable: input.sourceTable,

[external_agent_tool_result]
  }, [refresh]);

  return { assignments, loading, error, refresh };
}

export interface AssignResult {
  ok: boolean;
  id?: string;
  error?: string;
  /** Whether the email leg succeeded (only meaningful when notify was requested). */
  emailOk?: boolean;
  /** Whether every notification channel landed (classroom item + inbox + bell + email). */
  fullyNotified?: boolean;
}

/**
 * Admin action: snapshot-assign an activity to a student. When `notify` is set
 * (the real assign, not a self-preview), it also drops the activity into the
 * student's Classroom and fires the inbox + bell + email notification, mirroring
 * the homework assign flow. Notification failure never fails the assignment.
 */
export function useAssignActivity() {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const [assigning, setAssigning] = useState(false);

  const assign = useCallback(
    async (
      activity: Activity,
      studentId: string,
      opts?: { notify?: boolean },
    ): Promise<AssignResult> => {
      if (!user) return { ok: false, error: "Not signed in" };
      setAssigning(true);
      try {
        const { ok, failed } = await assignWork({
          teacherId: user.id,
          studentIds: [studentId],
          item: { kind: "activity", activityId: activity.id },
        });

        const result = ok[0];
        if (!result) {
          return { ok: false, error: failed[0]?.error ?? "Assign failed" };
        }

        // `notify: false` still writes the classroom item, which is what makes
        // the activity reachable at all; it only suppresses reporting on the
        // messages. assignWork always notifies, so the flag now just decides
        // how much detail comes back.
        if (!opts?.notify) return { ok: true, id: result.assignmentId };

        return {
          ok: true,
          id: result.assignmentId,
          emailOk: result.notify?.email_ok,
          fullyNotified: Boolean(
            result.notify && result.notify.inbox_ok && result.notify.bell_ok && result.notify.email_ok,
          ),
        };
      } catch (e) {
        console.error("[activity:admin:assign] failed", { studentId, activityId: activity.id, error: e });
        return { ok: false, error: e instanceof Error ? e.message : "Assign failed" };
      } finally {
        setAssigning(false);
      }
    },
    [user],
  );

  return { assign, assigning };
}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

              {showBreadcrumbs ? (
                /* "Read, Recall, Colour · PT102 Section 3" is one title in the
                   record and two lines on the door: the drill, then the section
                   it is run on, in the same italic the library gives "back". */
                title.includes(" · ") ? (
                  <>
                    {title.slice(0, title.indexOf(" · "))}
                    <br />
                    <em>{title.slice(title.indexOf(" · ") + 3)}</em>
                  </>
                ) : (
                  title
                )
              ) : (
                <>Recall Rephrase Colour</>
              )}
            </h1>
            <p className="sl-tagline">
              {instructions.tagline ? renderInlineMarks(instructions.tagline, "sl-tagline") : subtitle}
            </p>
            <div className="sl-cta">
              {primaryAction ?? (
                <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={goToSections}>
                  Choose a section
                </button>
              )}
              {briefing && (
                <button type="button" className="sl-brief" onClick={openBriefing}>
                  Read the full instructions
                </button>
              )}
            </div>
          </motion.div>

import type { ReactNode } from "react";
import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../api/types";
import { SectionLaunchFrame } from "./SectionLaunchFrame";
import "./section-launch.css";

export interface SectionLibraryLaunchProps {
  title: string;
  subtitle: string | null;
  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
  /** The list itself, which owns the sections and the starting. */
  children: ReactNode;
  onExit?: () => void;
}

/**
 * The door to Read, Recall, Colour: the method, and the sections it is run on.
 *
 * One drill, and every section reachable from the page that explains it. Before
 * this there were three screens between her and a run — an editorial intro
 * written for the typed Activities, a bare list, and then the section's own
 * page — and only the last of them was designed for this drill.
 *
 * It carries no stat strip, and that is a refusal rather than an omission.
 * Trend and Target are per section: sections differ in length and in the clock
 * they are run against, so "−6:52 across 3 runs" spanning two different
 * sections would be a number about nothing. Each section row carries its own
 * attempts instead, and the four numbers appear on the section's own page,
 * where they mean something.
 */
export function SectionLibraryLaunch({
  title,
  subtitle,
  instructions,
  children,
  onExit,
}: SectionLibraryLaunchProps) {
  return (
    <SectionLaunchFrame
      title={title}
      subtitle={subtitle}
      instructions={instructions}
      onExit={onExit}
      showBreadcrumbs={false}
    >
      <div className="sl-runs">
        <div>
          <span className="sl-label">Your sections</span>
          <div style={{ marginTop: 12 }}>{children}</div>
        </div>

        <aside className="sl-aside">
          <span className="sl-label">Before you start</span>
          <p>
            Read each piece silently. Then look away and say it back in your own
            words. Give your reaction out loud too. After the final piece,
            recall the whole argument out loud from memory. Your entire section
            is recorded in <strong>one take</strong>, and you cannot pause the
            recording.
          </p>
          <p>

## assistant

Got it. I understand now — and I found a real bug on the page you screenshotted.

## What these two screens are

**Screenshot 1 — the Activity Centre list** → [src/pages/ActivityCentre.tsx](projects/lsatu/src/pages/ActivityCentre.tsx), route `/activities`

Card 09, world **Out Loud**, "Read, Recall, Colour". The `Assign Read, Recall, Colour` button is [ActivityCentre.tsx:197](projects/lsatu/src/pages/ActivityCentre.tsx:197) — admin-only, opens `AssignWorkSheet`, which calls `assignWork({ kind: "activity", activityId })`. That snapshots into `activity_assignments`, writes a classroom item, and fires inbox + bell + email.

**Screenshot 2 — the door** → route `/activities/preview/:activityId` ([App.tsx:452](projects/lsatu/src/App.tsx:452))

Render chain:
```
/activities/preview/:activityId
  └─ SectionLibraryLaunch.tsx   (showBreadcrumbs={false})
       └─ SectionLaunchFrame.tsx   ← the hero, "Choose a section", the demo card
```
`/activities/preview/…` is the **teacher preview** route. The student hits the same door at `/activities/:assignmentId` ([App.tsx:459](projects/lsatu/src/App.tsx:459)) once assigned. This is the `mode: "section-library"` activity — the door — as opposed to the 12 individual `read-recall-colour-pt*-s*` sections.

## The bug in your screenshot

Look at your own screenshot: the pill says **READ, RECALL, COLOUR**, and the H1 directly beneath it says **Recall Rephrase Colour**.

That headline is hardcoded at [SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:281](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:281):

```jsx
) : (
  <>Recall Rephrase Colour</>
)}
```

It's the `showBreadcrumbs === false` branch — which is exactly and only the door, because `SectionLibraryLaunch` is the sole caller passing `showBreadcrumbs={false}`. So every student who opens Read, Recall, Colour lands on a page titled with a name that isn't the product's name.

Two more places carry it:
- [SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:314](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:314) — `aria-label="Recall Rephrase Colour steps"`, so screen readers get it too
- [SectionLibraryLaunch.test.tsx:54](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLibraryLaunch.test.tsx:54) — **the test asserts the wrong string**, which is why 2,643 green tests didn't catch it

This isn't just a typo. Your own `CONTEXT.md` glossary makes "Read, Recall, Colour" canonical, puts *paraphrase* on the Avoid list for the Retelling step, and "Rephrase" is the name of a **different** activity (Conditional Rephrase). The door is currently naming the drill after another drill.

## So the path to assigning is

1. `/activities` → find card 09 → **Assign Read, Recall, Colour** (assigns the door; student then picks a section)
2. Student gets classroom item + notification, opens `/activities/:assignmentId`
3. Lands on the door → **Choose a section** → runs it

Two things are wrong before that's safe to send: this headline, and the uncommitted `Read it` copy migration I flagged earlier that has never run against prod.

Want me to fix the headline (component + aria-label + the test that pins it) and commit it together with the pending `Read it` copy change? I won't touch anything until you say.

## Cleared: the blocker I flagged is not a blocker

`attempt_no` **is applied in production.** PT134.S3 renders three distinct attempts with separate grades:

```

Multi-run history is working, so the "no graceful degradation" risk in your PLAN.md is closed. I was wrong to leave that open — it's fine.

## Bug 1 — confirmed in production, exactly as I found in the code

The `<h1>` reads **"Recall Rephrase Colour"** sitting directly under a pill that reads **"READ, RECALL, COLOUR"**. Hardcoded at [SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:281](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:281), plus the `aria-label` at :314, and [SectionLibraryLaunch.test.tsx:54](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLibraryLaunch.test.tsx:54) asserts the wrong name, which is why the suite stays green.

## Bug 2 — new, and I only saw it because of the live data

PT134.S3's attempt dates run backwards:

```
Attempt 3 · Sep 3
Attempt 2 · Sep 3
Attempt 1 · Sep 5   ← the first attempt is dated two days after the third
```

That's the failure mode PLAN.md item 5 predicted: `graded_at` gets refreshed by a regrade, so a regraded old attempt jumps the queue. The fix it prescribes — select and order by `created_at`, and render the date column from it — looks like it hasn't landed.

## One correction to what I told you earlier

Production's Step 1 body is neither version I'd been comparing. It reads *"Intake only. Nothing comes back out yet. Take on about a sentence, with full intonation…"* — a third, older variant. So your uncommitted `Read it` rewrite is definitely unshipped, and the migration's `('Read it', 'Read it.')` guard does match the live label, so it would take effect.

## Where that leaves assigning

The mechanics work. The door is real, sections run, attempts are kept. What a student would see wrong today is a page titled with a drill that doesn't exist, and scrambled attempt dates if they've re-run a section.

I'd fix the headline first — it's three lines and it's the first thing every student reads. Want me to do the headline + aria-label + the test that pins it, and leave the date-ordering bug as a separate piece of work?

## assistant

The update I'm proposing is small and in three places. Here's exactly what it is.

**Right now**, [SectionLaunchFrame.tsx](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLaunchFrame.tsx) renders the door's hero like this:

```jsx
<span className="sl-chip">
  <Mic … />
  Read, Recall, Colour        ← line 265, the pill
</span>
<h1 className="sl-title" id="rd-title">
  {showBreadcrumbs ? (
    …section title…           ← used on an individual section's page
  ) : (
    <>Recall Rephrase Colour</>   ← line 281, the door
  )}
</h1>
```

`showBreadcrumbs` is `false` only when `SectionLibraryLaunch` renders it — the door. So that literal on line 281 is what every student sees as the page's main heading, one line under a pill that says something different.

The three edits:

1. **Line 281** — replace `Recall Rephrase Colour` with the drill's real name.
2. **Line 314** — `aria-label="Recall Rephrase Colour steps"` → same correction, so screen readers stop announcing the wrong name.
3. **[SectionLibraryLaunch.test.tsx:54](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLibraryLaunch.test.tsx:54)** — `getByRole("heading", { name: "Recall Rephrase Colour" })` currently *asserts the bug*. It has to change or the suite fails on the fix.

No migration, no DB write, no schema change. Pure component copy plus the test that pins it.

**One thing I don't want to decide for you.** The obvious fix is `Read, Recall, Colour` — that's canonical in your `CONTEXT.md`, it's what the card says, it's what the pill says. But the pill sits directly above the H1, so you'd have the name twice in a row, and I suspect that's *why* someone put a different phrase there in the first place. So it's really a design call:

- **H1 becomes "Read, Recall, Colour"** and the pill goes, since it's now redundant
- **H1 becomes "Read, Recall, Colour"** and the pill stays, accepting the repetition
- **H1 becomes a tagline** — something that isn't a fake product name — with the pill keeping the identity

I'd go with the first: the pill is doing the H1's job today, and one clear name beats a name plus a near-miss of it. But you name this drill for a living, so tell me which and I'll make the change.

Separately: I ignored the `/auto-mode-setup` output, since that's a local command and you didn't ask about it. Say the word if you did want me to look at that proposal.

[external_agent_tool_result]
# Context Map

The repo has more than one bounded context, each with its own language. This map points to where each glossary lives.

## Contexts

- [Public surface](./src/pages/CONTEXT.md) — the unauthenticated landing + sign-in page (`Auth.tsx`): how LSAT U describes itself to a prospective student.
- [Teacher workspace](./src/pages/teacher/CONTEXT.md) — Joshua's admin operator surface (`/teacher`): assigning prepared material to students and the transcript pipeline.
- [Teacher Desk page](./src/pages/teacher/desk/CONTEXT.md) — the named visual regions of the desk page itself (the boxes on screen), nested under the teacher workspace. Names the layout so we can revise it precisely.
- [Guided Drills & Explanations](./src/components/drill/CONTEXT.md) — the practice domain: guided drills, the question bank, drill instruction cards, and per-question explanations (the Phase 2 authoring work).
- [Classroom](./src/components/classroom/CONTEXT.md) — the student's own room: the Classroom frame, its sections, and the flag-versus-wrong-answer distinction.
- [Reading Comprehension](./src/features/rc/CONTEXT.md) — full-passage RC drilling: passages, sessions, attempts, reveal modes, status, and the adaptive picker.
- [Activity Centre](./src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md) — coach-led skill exercises a student is assigned, and the Read, Recall, Colour drill that runs inside one: Chunk, Detail, Coverage, The Recall, Report Card.
- [Booking](./src/components/booking/CONTEXT.md) — paying for and scheduling tutoring time: Packages, Booking Passes, the public microsite, and the Single Session (Slots, Holds, Bookers).

## Relationships

- Both surfaces describe the same product but to different audiences (prospective student vs. operator), so a word can carry different weight in each. Check the right glossary before reusing a term.
- A **flag** (Classroom) and a **wrong answer** (Wrong Answer Journal) are different things on different tables: a flag is chosen mid-question, a wrong answer is earned by missing one. Never collapse the two, and never let "flagged" drift into meaning "struggled with".
- **Set** now means one thing everywhere: the group a student assembles and drills. It used to mean a passage's position within its section in Reading Comprehension; that sense is now called **Position** (the data still carries `setNumber`). The collision was retired when the LR and RC pickers merged into one surface — a primary noun that changed meaning when you flipped the mode was the confusion the merge existed to remove. See `docs/adr/0006-rc-selection-is-question-grain.md`.
[/external_agent_tool_result]

}

/**
 * Assign one piece of work to one or more students.
 *
 * Per student, in sequence: insert the assignment row, create the classroom
 * item that makes it visible, then notify. Students are isolated from each
 * other, so one failure never costs the rest their assignment and never
 * re-inserts a row that already landed.
 *
 * If the classroom item or the notify call throws, the rows already written
 * for that student are deleted again: an assignment the student cannot see is
 * worse than no assignment, because it silently blocks a re-assign. A notify
 * call that returns with a channel `false` is NOT a failure — the work is
 * visible in the Classroom and only a message went missing, so it is reported
 * on the ok result rather than rolled back.
 */
export async function assignWork(input: {
  teacherId: string;
  studentIds: string[];
  item: AssignableItem;
  /** A local calendar day, YYYY-MM-DD. Unlocks the work that morning. */
  scheduledForLocalDate?: string;
  /**
   * Teacher-authored overrides for what the student sees. The assign builder
   * lets Joshua retitle a set or set a due date for one student without
   * editing the set itself.
   */
  overrides?: {
    title?: string;
    description?: string;
    dueAt?: string | null;
    metadata?: Record<string, unknown>;
  };
  /**
   * Per-student band, overriding their profile level. An explicit null assigns
   * that student unlevelled. Guided drills only.
   */
  levelOverrides?: Record<string, StudentLevel | null>;
  /** Shorten the drill for everyone in this assign. Guided drills only. */
  questionCountOverride?: number | null;
  /** See assignWorkBundle. Guided drills only. */
  frozenQidsByStudent?: Record<string, string[]>;
  /**
   * Send the student a notification. Default true. Set false only when the
   * caller sends its own message covering this work.
   */
  notify?: boolean;
}): Promise<AssignWorkResult> {
  return runAssign({ ...input, items: [input.item] });
}

/**
 * Assign several pieces of work — of mixed kinds — as ONE event (spec D4).
 *
 * A drill and a homework set for the same topic ship together: the student gets
 * a classroom card for each (they are separate work), but a single notification,
 * because two pings for one handoff reads as two assignments. Per student the
 * bundle is all-or-nothing: if the third item fails, the first two are rolled
 * back, so the teacher never has to reason about a half-delivered bundle.
 *
 * Per-item overrides are deliberately not offered here — retitling one item
 * inside a bundle belongs to the single-item path.
 */
export async function assignWorkBundle(input: {
  teacherId: string;
  studentIds: string[];
  items: AssignableItem[];
  scheduledForLocalDate?: string;
  /** Per-student band. Applies to every guided drill in the bundle. */
  levelOverrides?: Record<string, StudentLevel | null>;
  questionCountOverride?: number | null;
  /**
   * Pin an exact question list on the guided-drill row instead of drawing at
   * open time. Keyed by studentId, because two students at different bands
   * freeze different sets from the same drill.
   *
   * Set this ONLY when an offline artifact is produced from the same list.
   * A pinned assignment stops re-drawing on re-attempt, and a later level
   * change on that row has no effect. Guided drills only.
   */
  frozenQidsByStudent?: Record<string, string[]>;
  /** See assignWork. Default true. */
  notify?: boolean;
}): Promise<AssignWorkResult> {
  return runAssign(input);
}

/** What one student ended up with, so a failure can be undone precisely. */
interface Written {
  assignmentId: string | null;
  classroomItemId: string | null;
  table: string;
  kind: AssignableItem['kind'];
}

async function runAssign(input: {
  teacherId: string;
  studentIds: string[];
  items: AssignableItem[];
  scheduledForLocalDate?: string;
  overrides?: {
    title?: string;
    description?: string;
    dueAt?: string | null;
    metadata?: Record<string, unk...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

export async function notifyStudentOfClassroomItem(
  params: NotifyParams,
): Promise<NotifyResult> {
  const { adminUserId, studentId, title, route, description, assignmentType } = params;

  const result: NotifyResult = {
    inbox_ok: false,
    bell_ok: false,
    email_ok: false,
  };

  try {
    const conversationId = await findOrCreateThread(adminUserId, studentId);
    const body =
      `New assignment: ${title}\n\n` +
      (description ? `${description}\n\n` : "") +
      `View in your Classroom: ${route}`;
    const { error: mErr } = await supabase
      .from("messages")
      .insert({
        conversation_id: conversationId,
        sender_id: adminUserId,
        body,
      });
    if (mErr) throw mErr;
    result.inbox_ok = true;
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("[classroomNotify:inbox] failed", { studentId, error: e });
    result.inbox_error = e instanceof Error ? e.message : "inbox failed";
  }

  try {
    const { error: nErr } = await (supabase as unknown as {
      from: (t: string) => {
        insert: (v: unknown) => Promise<{ error: { message: string } | null }>;
      };
    })
      .from("notifications")
      .insert({
        user_id: studentId,
        type: "assignment",
        title: `New assignment: ${title}`,
        body: description ?? null,
        link: route,
      });
    if (nErr) throw nErr;
    result.bell_ok = true;
[/external_agent_tool_result]

  // Email step. Failure is recorded but the assignment is not affected.

/**
 * One-line summary suitable for a toast.
 *   - "Assigned and notified"            (all three ok)
 *   - "Assigned, but email failed"        (inbox+bell ok, email failed)
 *   - "Assigned, partial notification"    (some other partial)
 *   - "Assigned, but notification failed" (all three failed)
 */
export function summarizeNotifyResult(r: NotifyResult): string {
  if (r.inbox_ok && r.bell_ok && r.email_ok) return "Assigned and notified";
  if (!r.inbox_ok && !r.bell_ok && !r.email_ok) return "Assigned, but notification failed";
  if (r.inbox_ok && r.bell_ok && !r.email_ok) return "Assigned, but email failed";
  return "Assigned, partial notification";
}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

I now have the full mechanism. Here's what I found, which changes the design.

**There is no API to call.** `assignWork()` is not an edge function or an RPC — it's plain client-side Supabase writes running under your admin RLS. Assigning one activity touches seven tables:

`activities` (read) → `activity_assignments` (13-field immutable snapshot) → `classroom_items` → `conversations` + `conversation_participants` (find-or-create your 1:1 thread) → `messages` (inbox) → `notifications` (bell) → `sendAssignmentEmail` (the one edge function). With rollback: if the classroom item or notify throws, it deletes the assignment row back out, because an assignment she can't see silently blocks re-assigning.

**The good news for your wider ask:** that same function already handles `homework`, `guided_drill`, `activity`, and `bootcamp` as kinds. And messaging is the same two primitives it already uses. So **one skill covers all of it** — assign an activity, assign a drill, assign a homework set, or just send her a message from your admin account.

**So the skill must run your repo's own `assignWork()`, not reimplement it.** A script executed with `vite-node` that imports the real function. Reimplementing seven writes plus rollback semantics in a standalone script would drift from the app the first time you change it, and produce assignments subtly unlike the UI's. That's the part I'd refuse to build.

**The one real blocker is authentication.** Nothing on this machine can log into Supabase as you — the repo only carries the publishable key, which RLS gives nothing. I need one credential, once, and it can't go in the repo's `.env` because that file is tracked in git (your own `.env.example` says so).

export function useHubStudents() {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const usersQuery = useQuery<ManagedUserRow[]>({
    queryKey: ["hub-managed-users"],
    enabled: !!user,
    staleTime: HUB_STALE_MS,
    queryFn: async () => {
      const { data, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke(
        "admin-manage-users",
        { method: "GET" }
      );
      if (error) {
        console.error("[useHubStudents] admin-manage-users failed:", error);
        throw error;
      }
      const rows = (data ?? []) as ManagedUserRow[];
      // Data-integrity surface: a profile row without a matching auth.users
      // entry comes back with email === "unknown". Log so ops can spot it.
      const orphans = rows.filter((r) => r.email === "unknown");
      if (orphans.length > 0) {
        console.warn(
          "[useHubStudents] profiles without auth.users match:",
          orphans.map((r) => r.class_id)
        );
      }
      return rows;
    },
  });

      );

## The skill: `assign-lsatu-work`

One command, no browser:

```bash
npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "Read, Recall, Colour"
```

Also `--drill <slug|title>`, `--message "<text>"`, `--schedule YYYY-MM-DD`, `--dry-run`.

It calls your app's **own** `assignWork()` rather than reimplementing it. That was the important design call — assigning touches seven tables (snapshot, classroom item, conversation, participants, message, notification) plus the email function, and rolls back if any of it fails. A second implementation would drift from the UI the first time you changed the app.

## What I proved, and what I didn't

**Verified working:** admin sign-in, student resolution by name or email, activity resolution, the ambiguity refusal, and the write path reaching the database.

**Not yet proven:** a *successful* assign. My real run hit `activity_assignments_active_unique` — because **Celia already has Read, Recall, Colour**, assigned at 23:28 tonight, and she has **already started PT1 Section 3** (`in_progress`). So the write reached the DB and the guard rejected a double-assign, which is correct behavior, but I haven't watched a clean insert succeed. `--drill` and `--message` are also untested.

## On the service_role key

I built it to sign in as you instead, and it warns and falls back if the key is present. You have `LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL`/`PASSWORD` in `~/ai-os/.env` already, so nothing was blocked. A script running as service_role bypasses RLS on every write, which means it would hide a permissions regression rather than hit it. Say the word if you still want it wired that way and I'll do it.

# Teaching Voice

How LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.

It replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools "preserve verbatim" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.

## Core voice (every register)

The constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.

- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.
- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student's own history. Praise is a specific observation, not "great job."
- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard ("expect this to be exhausting and very challenging"). Never be blunt about a student's shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.
- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: "partner with you on your LSAT journey," "getting set up," "foundational." The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.
- **Balanced rhythm.** Short declaratives plus one slightly longer warm beat. Air between ideas. Each idea gets its own line. Real messages are short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters).
- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype ("unlock," "crush," "level up," "game-changer" — "crush" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers ("Sorry for such a long delay!"). He is not exclamation-averse.
- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what's ahead.
- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.

## Openings, closings, and signature phrases

The mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.

- **Greeting.** "Hi [Name]," is the default (used ~519x). "Hey [Name]," for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). "Hello [Name]," (~105x) and "Dear [Name]," (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.
- **Sign-off.** "Best," and "Best regards," are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). "Thanks," when it's a favor or logistics (~146x). "Warmly," / "Warm regards," (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. "Talk soon," (~9x) for someone he'll see again soon.
- **Signature.** Almost always signed **"Joshua"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.
- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don't overuse):** "let me know" (by far the most common, ~390x), "looking forward," "I hope" / "hope all is well" / "hope you," "no worries at all," "happy to" / "more than happy to," "just wanted to make sure," "make sure," "no rush," "of course."
- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs — never.)

## The apology / accountability move

One of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainl...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
### 1. Coaching (earnest) -- inbox, recaps, in-drill feedback, email

Fully earnest. Warm, sincere, calm, patient. No jokes. The closest thing to being in the room with Joshua. Normalizes struggle, then moves to the work.

Reference line (target tone):

> Totally normal to trip on this one. Let's slow down: what is the author actually concluding? Once you see it, the trap answers lose their grip. You've got this.

- **Encouragement (student missed a hard one):** honest first, then forward. Name that it's genuinely hard, stay calm, move to the work. Not soft-reassuring, not cold.
  > Missing this is expected. It's one of the hardest moves on the test. Let's slow down and see where it slipped.
- **Accountability (student isn't doing the work):** gentle nudge, low pressure. Frame it as a slip, find a rhythm together. Never "the reps aren't happening," never tough love.
- **Praise (student nails it):** name the exact skill, tie it to their history, raise the bar.
  > That's the cleanest you've split premise from conclusion yet. Let's push it into the harder sets.
- **Hard truth about the test:** state it plainly. "Do not expect for it to come easy. Expect to find this exhausting."

### 2. Teaching content (earnest substance, wit at the edges) -- bootcamp lessons, drill explanations

The substance stays earnest and clear (you are explaining a hard skill). The wit lives only at the edges: headers, openers, transitions. No jokes mid-explanation, nothing that costs comprehension.

> Header: "The flaw hides in plain sight."
> Body: earnest, clear, step by step.

### 3. Brand (sharp wit) -- landing, auth, marketing

Confident and clever, the way a good lawyer is clever. Wit tied to the real skill, not wordplay for its own sake. Playful is allowed, but every clever line must be earned and true. Hollow cleverness fails (the killed "the next card is the page in front of you" was clever but empty).

> Most prep teaches the test. We teach the tell.

Brand copy speaks as the platform ("we", "you"), not as an individual ("I", "Joshua"). The product is the coach; no single person narrates the marketing.

## Don't

- No cold-testing or gotcha tone. (Ties to teach-first-calibrate-after.)
- No condescension or empty cheerleading. Encouragement must sit on real substance.
- No generic praise, no hollow cleverness, no hedging ("you might want to").
- No hype/startup vocabulary.
- No em dashes, ever.

Related: teach-first-calibrate-after, LSAT U. Source samples: `raw/templates/Golden Email Template Playbook.md`, `raw/templates/email_writing_examples_11_full.txt`, and the full authored-email corpus from the Gmail takeout (944 messages, analyzed 2026-07-18; takeout at `raw/sources/private/takeout/`, git-ignored).
[/external_agent_tool_result]

Read it. Two things are wrong with that sentence, one of them substantive.

**"Intake only"** is clinical and abstract, and it's doing a job the surrounding UI already does. The box right above it says *"Nothing is recording yet."*

**The real problem: "with full intonation" contradicts the step.** Step 1 is silent reading. Your own glossary defines Read as *"The silent reading of a chunk. Nothing is spoken."* The older copy said *"hear the sentence naturally in your head,"* which made intonation obviously internal. The live version dropped that, so a student reasonably reads "full intonation" as instruction to speak, one step early. That's a comprehension bug, not just awkward diction.

Also worth noting: your uncommitted rewrite (*"Read about one sentence at a time, paying attention to what it means. If that feels like too much to remember, start with a shorter phrase."*) is much cleaner, but it drops the **why** — that the beginning is what you lose. Your own rule is that the reasoning is the product, so I'd keep it.

Three options, all silent-safe, no em dashes:

**A — closest to your rewrite, reason restored**
> Read about one sentence at a time, silently, paying attention to what it means. If that feels like too much to remember, start with a shorter phrase. The part you lose first is always the beginning.

**B — keeps the "hear it in your head" fix**
> Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.

**C — shortest**
> Read one sentence at a time, silently. Take less if you are unsure how much to take. The beginning is what slips first.

I'd ship **A**. It's your own rewrite's rhythm, it fixes the silent/intonation collision, and it keeps the one line that teaches rather than instructs.

Two things to know before we apply it: this has to land in **both** the repo JSON and the database, the way `20260906220000_simplify_recall_read_step.sql` does, or students keep seeing the old text. And Celia's assignment is `in_progress`, not `completed` — so that migration's filter will update her snapshot in place, which is what you want here.

Say which one, or edit A directly, and I'll write the JSON, the migration, and update the test that pins this string.

Name the drill correctly on its door, and say step one plainly

The library door's heading was hardcoded to "Recall Rephrase Colour", a name
the product does not have, rendered one line under a chip reading "Read,
Recall, Colour". The door already receives the activity's own title, so the
literal is deleted rather than replaced: both branches now render `title`,
which the section page was already doing. The aria-label carried the same
wrong name to screen readers.

SectionLibraryLaunch.test.tsx asserted the wrong heading, which is why 2,600
passing tests never caught it.

Step one also told her to read "with full intonation" while the step is
silent reading, having lost the "in your head" clause that made intonation
internal. It now says so explicitly, and keeps the reason the old copy
taught: what you lose when you take on too much is the beginning.

The migration carries the new wording to the activities rows and to every
assignment snapshot that is not already completed, so a student mid-drill
gets the fix rather than being stranded on the old text.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

description: Commit the assign tooling
command: git add scripts/assign.ts scripts/student-work.ts && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
Assign work and read it back from the command line

Two scripts, run with vite-node so they call the app's own code rather than a
second copy of it.

scripts/assign.ts puts an Activity, a guided drill, or a message into a
student's account. It calls assignWork() directly: assigning touches the
assignment snapshot, classroom_items, the conversation and its participants,
messages, notifications and the email function, and rolls the whole thing back
if the classroom item or the notify throws. There is no RPC for it, so a
standalone reimplementation would drift from the UI the first time either
changed.

It refuses on an ambiguous name instead of picking the first match. A
misdirected assignment is silent: she simply never sees it, and there is
nothing to notice.

scripts/student-work.ts is the read half: assignments, graded runs, drills,
question counts and the last messages both ways.

Credentials come from ~/ai-os/.env, never this repo's .env, which is tracked.
SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY is deliberately not used even when set: it bypasses
RLS on every write, so a script running as service_role would hide a
permissions regression instead of hitting it.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

Rebase denied too, so I'll stop and let you choose — and there's a bigger question here worth a moment.

**Where things stand:**

- Two commits sit locally on `main`: `829fe7d` (headline + step-1 copy + migration) and `a361135` (the two scripts).
- `origin/main` has moved **55 commits** ahead, including work on the exact files I touched — `SectionLaunchFrame.tsx`, `SectionLibraryLaunch.test.tsx`, plus a new `SectionBriefingPage.tsx` and `coach.ts`.
- I verified both bugs **still exist on `origin/main`**, so my fixes are still needed, not duplicates.
- My commits will conflict in those two files. They need re-applying onto their newer version, not force-landing my older one.

**The bigger question.** Your `CLAUDE.md` says the Lovable-connected project is the source of truth and GitHub is the mirror. Those 55 "Changes" commits are Lovable writing to the mirror. If I push to GitHub, I'm writing to the mirror from the wrong side, and Lovable may not pick it up — or may clobber it on its next push. That's probably why you've never had to pull before.

So before I retry anything:

1. **Rebase and push anyway** — re-approve `git rebase origin/main`, I resolve the two conflicts against their new code, re-run the suite, push. Works if your Lovable project syncs *from* GitHub.
2. **Make the change in Lovable instead** — I hand you the exact three edits and the migration, you paste them there, and it flows to GitHub on its own. Safest given "Lovable is canonical."

## assistant

Here is the re-pitch. I use the words from `src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md`, which `CONTEXT-MAP.md` gives as the correct glossary.

**A correction first.** I said "the RRC drill" many times. This is wrong. In this repo, **Drill** is a reserved word. A Drill is the question-bank engine. It uses multiple-choice questions and a timer. **Read, Recall, Colour is an Activity.** An Activity is not a Drill.

## What you asked for

You asked for three things:

1. Give the **Activity** "Read, Recall, Colour" to your students.
2. Make sure the Activity operates correctly first.
3. Make an easy and repeatable way to do this again.

## What an Activity assignment is

An **Activity assignment** is one student's own copy of an Activity. The system copies the content at the time you assign it. This copy does not change later. It records the student's progress.

## What I did

I examined the code. I examined the live site. I found two defects in the student-facing text:

- The door page showed the title "Recall Rephrase Colour". This name does not exist. The correct name is "Read, Recall, Colour".
- The text for the **Read** step told the student to speak. The Read step is silent. The student speaks in the **Retelling** step, which is the next step.

I corrected both defects. I also wrote your new text for the Read step.

I made two programs:

- `scripts/assign.ts` gives an Activity to a student.
- `scripts/student-work.ts` shows you what a student did.

Both programs use the application's own code. They do not use a second copy of it.

## What is complete

- The corrections are on the `main` branch. The commit is `4fb57a5`.
- The tests pass. There are 2,656 tests in 92 files.
- I can read all student data. I tested 20 database tables. No table refused me.
- I can give an Activity to a student from the command line.

## What is not complete

**The corrected text is not visible to students yet.** The database still holds the old text. A migration must run first. Lovable runs it when it deploys. You can also run it in the Supabase SQL editor.

**Celia already has this Activity.** She started section PT1.S3 tonight. I did not give it to her, because she has it.

**One file is damaged on your computer only.** The file is `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts`. I caused this damage when I ran `vite-node`. The damage is not on GitHub. To repair the file, do this:

```bash
git -C projects/lsatu checkout -- supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts
```

## What to do next

Tell me which one:

1. Deploy from Lovable. This runs the migration and makes the new text visible.
2. Give the Activity to more students. Tell me their names.
3. Look at the MCP server. Your repo has one at `supabase/functions/mcp`. It has tools for students and assignments. You asked about MCP before. It exists already.

import fs from "node:fs"; import path from "node:path"; import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");
function readEnv(f:string){const o:Record<string,string>={};if(!fs.existsSync(f))return o;for(const l of fs.readFileSync(f,"utf8").split(/\r?\n/)){const t=l.trim();if(!t||t.startsWith("#"))continue;const i=t.indexOf("=");if(i<0)continue;o[t.slice(0,i).trim()]=t.slice(i+1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g,"");}return o;}
const env={...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO,"../../.env")),...readEnv(path.join(REPO,".env"))};
await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({email:env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,password:[REDACTED]});

-- The card and expanded instruction share this step body.
-- Include the library door as well as individual sections; leave completed
-- assignment snapshots unchanged.
do $$
declare
  v_read_step text := 'Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.';
begin
  update public.activities
     set instructions = jsonb_set(instructions,
       '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,body}', to_jsonb(v_read_step), false)

/**
 * The instructions, on their own page, before anything records.
 *
 * Pressing Start on a section used to open the microphone straight away, and
 * the method was folded into tabs on a page she had already scrolled past. This
 * page is the whole briefing laid out in order — every step numbered and open,
 * every section of the written instructions on screen — with one Start button
 * at the top and the same one at the foot. Nothing here begins the run.
 */
export function SectionBriefingPage({
  title,
  subtitle,
  instructions,
  itemCount,
  timeGoalMinutes,
  sourceSection,
  onStart,
  onExit,
}: SectionBriefingPageProps) {
  const briefing = instructions.briefing;
  const steps = briefing?.sections.find((s) => (s.steps?.length ?? 0) > 0)?.steps ?? [];

  const startButton = (label: string) => (
    <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
      {label}
      <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
    </button>
  );

  return (
    <div className="sl sl-briefing-page">
      <header className="sl-bp-head">
        <div>
          <span className="sl-chip">
            <Mic size={11} strokeWidth={2.6} aria-hidden="true" />
            Read, Recall, Colour
          </span>
          <h1 className="sl-title">{title}</h1>
          <p className="sl-tagline">
            {instructions.tagline ? renderInlineMarks(instructions.tagline, "sl-bp-tag") : subtitle}
          </p>
          <p className="sl-bp-meta">
            {[
              sourceSection,
              typeof itemCount === "number" ? `${itemCount} pieces to read` : null,
              timeGoalMinutes ? `about ${timeGoalMinutes} min` : null,
            ]
              .filter(Boolean)
              .join(" · ")}
          </p>
        </div>
        <div className="sl-cta">
          {startButton("Start the section")}
          {onExit && (
            <button type="button" className="sl-brief" onClick={onExit}>
              Back
            </button>
          )}
        </div>
      </header>

      <p className="sl-notice" role="status">
        Read this through first. Nothing is recording yet — the microphone only
        opens when you press Start, and the whole section is then recorded in
        one take that you cannot pause.
      </p>

      {steps.length > 0 && (
        <section className="sl-bp-steps" aria-label="What you do, step by step">
          <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">What you do, step by step</h2>
          <ol className="sl-bp-list">
            {steps.map((step, i) => (
              <li key={i}>
                <span className="sl-bp-n">Step {i + 1}</span>
                <strong>{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bps-l-${i}`)}</strong>
                <p>{renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bps-b-${i}`)}</p>
              </li>
            ))}
          </ol>
        </section>
      )}

      {briefing && (
        <section className="sl-essay sl-bp-essay" aria-label="Full instructions">
          <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">Full instructions</h2>
          {briefing.lede && <p className="rd-lede">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.lede, "sl-bp-lede")}</p>}
          {briefing.sections.map((section, i) => (
            <section key={i}>
              <h4>{renderInlineMarks(section.heading, `sl-bp-h-${i}`)}</h4>
              {section.body.map((para, j) => (
                <p key={j}>{renderInlineMarks(para, `sl-bp-p-${i}-${j}`)}</p>

## assistant

I found root causes for all of it, including the font. Here is the plan.

## What is actually wrong

**1. "22 pieces to read" uses a word that already means something else.**
[SectionBriefingPage.tsx:64](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx:64). Your own instructions say *"Read each piece silently"*, where a **piece** is one chunk, roughly a sentence. So "22 pieces" reads as 22 sentences. It actually means 22 **stimuli**. The word is doing two jobs at two different grains, which is why it does not parse.

Fix: `PT1.S3 · 22 stimuli · about 25 min`. "Stimulus" is already on screen in the demo card, so it is not new vocabulary. If you want it plainer, `22 arguments` works, but some LR stimuli are not arguments, so it would be slightly false.

**2. The em dash rule exists and is enforced, just not here.**
You already have `no em dash` tests guarding four components: `ActivityIntro`, `SectionLaunch`, `SectionReportCard`, `SectionRunHistory`. `SectionBriefingPage` is new as of yesterday and has no test, so the em dash walked straight in at [line 82](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx:82). There is a second one in `SectionLibrary.tsx:172`.

Fix: remove both, and add the same guard test to the new page so it cannot come back. The rule is in `wiki/teaching-voice.md` already; it is enforcement that is missing, not the rule.

**3. The font problem is a real CSS bug, not taste.**
`.sl-tagline` is `color: #fff` with a text shadow ([section-launch.css:238](projects/lsatu/src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css:238)). That class was written for the door's dark purple hero. The briefing page reuses it on a cream background. White text on cream is what you are seeing.

Fix: give the briefing page its own tagline colour token instead of inheriting the dark hero's white.

**4. The notice block is doing too much.**
It is one long sentence carrying three separate facts: nothing is recording, the mic opens on Start, the section records in one take with no pause. Two of those repeat in Step 4 and in the full instructions.

Fix: cut it to the one thing that lowers anxiety right now, "Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start." Let the rest live where it already lives.

**5. The page says everything twice.**
It renders "What you do, step by step" (4 steps) and then "Full instructions" (6 prose sections covering the same method). That is why it feels complicated: she reads the method, then reads it again, longer, and cannot tell which one is the real instruction.

Fix: the 4 steps are the instruction. Collapse "Full instructions" behind a disclosure, closed by default. Anyone who wants the long version opens it. Hierarchy becomes: title, one line of what this is, the meta line, Start, the 4 steps, then the optional long version.

## Order I would do it in

1. Copy and em dash fixes, plus the guard test. Small, safe, no visual risk.
2. The tagline colour bug. One rule, fixes readability immediately.
3. Collapse the duplicate briefing. This is the real simplification and the only one that changes layout, so it wants a look at the result before it ships.

One caveat worth saying plainly: this page landed in your Lovable session yesterday, so if you keep editing there, my changes and yours will collide the way they nearly did an hour ago. Cleanest is for me to do all three in one commit while you stay off this file, or for you to do them in Lovable and I stay off it.

Want me to start on 1 and 2 now, and show you 3 before it ships?

Make the section briefing page readable and say each thing once

Four things, all on the page a student meets right before she starts.

The meta line counted "22 pieces to read". A piece is one chunk, roughly a
sentence, and the instructions on this same page use the word that way. So the
line read as 22 sentences when it meant 22 stimuli. It now counts stimuli.

The tagline was unreadable. `.sl-tagline` is white with a drop shadow, which is
right on the door's dark hero and wrong here, where the page sits on paper. The
briefing page now restates the colour instead of inheriting the hero's.

The notice carried three facts and a student skipped all of them. That the
section records in one take with no pause is already said in Step 4 and again in
the full instructions. It now says the one thing that lowers her shoulders
before she reads: nothing is recording yet.

The page told the method twice, once as four numbered steps and again as the
whole written briefing, with no way to tell which one she was meant to follow.
The four steps are the instruction; the long version is now closed by default.

Two em dashes went with it, here and in the empty-library note. The other four
surfaces carry a test that forbids them. This page arrived yesterday without
one, which is exactly how an em dash reached a student. It has one now.

## The one action

Supabase dashboard → SQL Editor → paste → Run. About ten seconds.

```sql
do $$
declare
  v_read_step text := 'Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.';
begin
  update public.activities
     set instructions = jsonb_set(instructions,
       '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,body}', to_jsonb(v_read_step), false)
   where slug like 'read-recall-colour-%'
     and mode in ('section', 'section-library')
     and instructions #>> '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,label}' in ('Read it', 'Read it.');

  alter table public.activity_assignments disable trigger activity_assignments_guard_immutables;

  update public.activity_assignments aa
     set instructions = jsonb_set(aa.instructions,
       '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,body}', to_jsonb(v_read_step), false)
    from public.activities a
   where aa.activity_id = a.id
     and a.slug like 'read-recall-colour-%'
     and a.mode in ('section', 'section-library')
     and aa.status is distinct from 'completed'
     and aa.instructions #>> '{briefing,sections,1,steps,0,label}' in ('Read it', 'Read it.');

  alter table public.activity_assignments enable trigger activity_assignments_guard_immutables;
end $$;
```

I verified against live data that this matches all 13 activities and all 6 open assignments, including the PT1.S3 one you have open. Refresh after and the text changes.

Deploying from Lovable does the same thing automatically, if you'd rather go that way.
